NOTICE.md:1: # Sources and attribution
NOTICE.md:2: 
NOTICE.md:3: This project combines publicly available datasets and original analysis.
NOTICE.md:4: 
NOTICE.md:5: - **U.S. Energy Information Administration (EIA):** EIA-861 annual utility reporting and EIA-930 hourly demand. https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia861/ and https://www.eia.gov/electricity/gridmonitor/
NOTICE.md:6: - **Catalyst Cooperative PUDL:** historical EIA-861 reliability, smart metering, and electricity-sales extracts, used under the stated **Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 (CC BY 4.0)** terms. https://docs.catalyst.coop/pudl/en/stable/data_access.html . Data is excerpted/standardized for this project; the analyses, joins and charts are project work.
NOTICE.md:7: - **NOAA NCEI Storm Events Database:** NOAA Storm Events details files, 2013–2025, with NWS zone-to-county lookup. https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/pub/data/swdi/stormevents/csvfiles/ and https://www.weather.gov/gis/ZoneCounty
NOTICE.md:8: - **Dominion Energy annual report and investor presentations:** referenced for published company context and numerical reconciliation, not copied into this repository as full documents.
NOTICE.md:9: 
NOTICE.md:10: For precise source files, versions and transformation details, see [docs/01_sources.md](docs/01_sources.md). No software license is declared for this repository by this attribution note; choosing a code license remains the owner's decision.
README.md:1: # Dominion Energy Virginia | Reliability review
README.md:2: 
README.md:3: **A public-data look at customer outages, severe weather and changing electricity demand.**
README.md:4: 
README.md:5: ## Why I did this
README.md:6: 
README.md:7: Dominion reported that its rolling three-year average SAIDI (outage minutes per customer) rose from about **130 minutes** (2022–2024) to **133 minutes** (2023–2025), excluding major event days. These are overlapping, rolling averages—not two separate three-year periods.
README.md:8: 
README.md:9: I wanted to look beyond the headline: **Were customers losing power more often, were interruptions lasting longer, and how much context could weather and demand data provide?**
README.md:10: 
README.md:11: ## What I found
README.md:12: 
README.md:13: ### 1. Interruption frequency is the more useful place to investigate
README.md:14: 
README.md:15: ![Change in Dominion reliability measures, 2023 to 2025](figures/reliability-change.svg)
README.md:16: 
README.md:17: *Chart recreated from the reported EIA-861 values*
README.md:18: 
README.md:19: From 2023 to 2025, Dominion Virginia's reported reliability measures (excluding major event days) changed as follows:
README.md:20: 
README.md:21: | Measure | 2023 | 2025 | Change |
README.md:22: |---|---:|---:|---:|
README.md:23: | Outage minutes per customer (SAIDI) | 123.3 | 144.4 | +17% |
README.md:24: | Interruptions per customer (SAIFI) | 1.165 | 1.320 | +13% |
README.md:25: | Average minutes per interruption (CAIDI) | 105.9 | 109.4 | +3% |
README.md:26: 
README.md:27: **My takeaway:** The rise in outage minutes was associated more with **more frequent interruptions** than with much longer interruptions. That doesn't tell us which equipment or circuits caused the change, but it gives us a better question to pursue.
README.md:28: 
README.md:29: ### 2. Weather helps explain the pattern, but the regression is exploratory
README.md:30: 
README.md:31: I grouped NOAA storm reports into **storm county-days**: one county with at least one relevant storm report on a given date counts once, even if NOAA recorded several individual events. For Dominion's Virginia service area, the count increased from **465 in 2024** to **796 in 2025**.
README.md:32: 
README.md:33: I compared annual storm exposure with SAIDI and fitted a simple linear regression using **2015–2024** data, holding 2025 back as a test.
README.md:34: 
README.md:35: - **Historical correlation:** 0.70 (R² about 0.48).
README.md:36: - **Model estimate for 2025:** 145.3 outage minutes per customer.
README.md:37: - **Actual reported 2025 value:** 144.4 minutes.
README.md:38: 
README.md:39: The close 2025 result is interesting, **not proof that storms caused the increase or that the model will predict other years accurately**. The fit uses only ten annual observations, doesn't control for infrastructure and maintenance, and compares all-year storm exposure with a reliability measure that excludes major event days. I treat the model-based weather adjustment (about 133 minutes for 2025) as an exploratory comparison, not a verified measure of underlying performance.
README.md:40: 
README.md:41: Separately, EIA reports **306.7 minutes with all events** versus **144.4 excluding major event days** in 2025. The difference reflects EIA's major-event reporting classification; it should not automatically be labelled as minutes caused by NOAA-recorded storms.
README.md:42: 
README.md:43: ### 3. Demand is changing even outside peak hours
README.md:44: 
README.md:45: ![Change in PJM DOM demand from 2019 to 2025](figures/demand-growth.svg)
README.md:46: 
README.md:47: *Chart recreated from EIA-930 figures; this is not a screenshot of Power BI.*
README.md:48: 
README.md:49: EIA-930 records for the broader **PJM DOM subregion** show that between 2019 and 2025:
README.md:50: 
README.md:51: - The highest hourly demand increased about **26%**.
README.md:52: - The lowest hourly demand increased about **52%**.
README.md:53: 
README.md:54: This suggests a changing load profile, not merely a higher annual peak. The DOM subregion is **not identical** to Dominion's Virginia retail territory, and these figures don't establish that demand growth caused outages.
README.md:55: 
README.md:56: ## Why it matters
README.md:57: 
README.md:58: If I were working with internal operational data, I would start by investigating **which feeders and outage causes are contributing to the rise in interruption frequency**. I'd look at repeat failures, customers affected, equipment condition, vegetation and maintenance history. That could help teams evaluate where preventive work might deliver the greatest reduction in customer outage minutes.
README.md:59: 
README.md:60: The public data helps narrow down the questions. **It cannot identify the right circuit to upgrade or prove which investment will work.**
README.md:61: 
README.md:62: ## How I approached it
README.md:63: 
README.md:64: I combined annual utility reliability data from **EIA-861** (historical years standardized by **PUDL**, plus the 2025 EIA release), storm records from **NOAA**, and hourly demand from **EIA-930**. I used SQL to make inconsistent formats comparable, check duplicates and missing values, calculate annual measures, and prepare the data for Power BI.
README.md:65: 
README.md:66: The technical implementation and checks are available below, but the focus of this review is **what the numbers mean and what I'd investigate next**.
README.md:67: 
README.md:68: ## How the analysis is organized
README.md:69: 
README.md:70: The work has three steps: **bring together the public reports, check and standardize the numbers in SQL, then explain the results in Power BI**. Each source addresses a different question; I did not merge weather, demand and reliability into one giant record.
README.md:71: 
README.md:72: | Material | Where to look |
README.md:73: | --- | --- |
README.md:74: | Source data and annual results | [data/](data/) |
README.md:75: | Cleaning, calculations and tests | [sql/](sql/) |
README.md:76: | Supporting explanations and audit trail | [docs/](docs/) |
README.md:77: | Power BI measures and workbook | [powerbi/](powerbi/) |
README.md:78: 
README.md:79: ## How to check the work
README.md:80: 
README.md:81: The core reliability tables can be recreated locally using the committed EIA/PUDL files:
README.md:82: 
README.md:83: ```bash
README.md:84: python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
README.md:85: python -I scripts/run_local_duckdb.py
README.md:86: ```
README.md:87: 
README.md:88: This runner covers the reliability SQL and its six checks (T1-T6). NOAA and EIA-930 are separate pipelines with six further SQL checks. The local runner does **not** reproduce the entire Power BI report or its 2025 weather fit.
README.md:89: 
README.md:90: The project includes the Power BI measure definitions and supporting workbook, but **not a .pbix dashboard file**. The two charts above are reproductions of selected findings from the reported figures, not exports from Power BI Desktop.
README.md:91: 
README.md:92: For data attribution, see [NOTICE.md](NOTICE.md) and [source notes](docs/01_sources.md). Detailed field mappings and known source anomalies are kept in [docs/](docs/) for reproducibility.
README.md:93: 
README.md:94: ## Explore the project
README.md:95: 
README.md:96: - [How to read the dashboard](docs/06_powerbi_report.md)
README.md:97: - [Data sources](docs/01_sources.md)
README.md:98: - [Worked examples from source rows to results](docs/08_transformations.md)
README.md:99: - [Data-quality checks](docs/03_data_quality.md)
README.md:100: - [SQL transformations and analysis](sql/)
README.md:101: - [Power BI measures](powerbi/measures.dax)
README.md:102: 
README.md:103: To reproduce the **core reliability** calculations without Snowflake, run `python scripts/run_local_duckdb.py`. The NOAA and hourly-demand analyses use the additional input files and SQL documented in `scripts/` and `sql/`. The Power BI report file itself is not included in the repository.
data/README.md:1: # Data files
data/README.md:2: 
data/README.md:3: The original data files are stored separately from the supporting files and the results of my analysis making it easier to trace a number in the dashboard back to where it came from.
data/README.md:4: 
data/README.md:5: ## What's in each folder?
data/README.md:6: 
data/README.md:7: | Folder | What it's for |
data/README.md:8: | --- | --- |
data/README.md:9: | [`input/`](input/) | Four EIA/PUDL extracts covering reliability, customer meters and electricity sales. The historical reliability file covers 2013–2024; the separate EIA file provides 2025. |
data/README.md:10: | [`reference/`](reference/) | Information used to interpret the data: Dominion's Virginia service counties, NOAA's weather-zone-to-county lookup, and selected figures from Dominion investor presentations. |
data/README.md:11: | [`output/`](output/) | Three exported summaries: annual storm exposure, storms alongside SAIDI, and annual demand for the PJM DOM subregion. |
data/README.md:12: 
data/README.md:13: ## How I used the files
data/README.md:14: 
data/README.md:15: **Reliability:** I put the 2013–2024 history and 2025 EIA records into a consistent annual dataset. I used this to check Dominion's reported SAIDI, SAIFI and CAIDI trends.
data/README.md:16: 
data/README.md:17: **Weather:** NOAA records are filed by event, but several reports may describe weather affecting the same county on the same day. I matched the events to Dominion's Virginia service area using the files in `reference/`, then counted each county-date once. The result is [`storm_index.csv`](output/storm_index.csv). I matched these yearly totals with reported outage minutes in [`storm_vs_saidi.csv`](output/storm_vs_saidi.csv) for an exploratory regression.
data/README.md:18: 
data/README.md:19: **Electricity demand:** I filtered EIA-930 hourly records to the PJM DOM subregion, checked unusual readings, and summarized demand by year in [`dom_annual.csv`](output/dom_annual.csv). The DOM subregion is broader than Dominion's Virginia retail service area.
data/README.md:20: 
data/README.md:21: **Meters and sales:** The PUDL files in `input/` helped me compare smart-meter adoption, electricity use and customer growth. Those analyses don't have separate CSV exports in `output/`.
data/README.md:22: 
data/README.md:23: ## Where are the full NOAA and hourly demand files?
data/README.md:24: 
data/README.md:25: They are too large to keep in this repository. The download scripts in [`scripts/`](../scripts/) retrieve them into a local `data/raw/` folder, which Git ignores. **So `raw/` won't appear in the GitHub folder listing.** The smaller files needed to reproduce the core reliability analysis are included here.
data/README.md:26: 
data/README.md:27: I didn't manually change numbers to make the charts. The cleaning and calculations are defined in [`sql/`](../sql/). For exact sources and file counts, see [Sources](../docs/01_sources.md); for worked examples showing how a source row becomes a result, see [Transformations](../docs/08_transformations.md).
data/README.md:28: 
data/README.md:29: **Output format note:** `data/output/dom_annual.csv` is a compact saved snapshot, not an exact export of every column in `MART.DOM_LOAD_ANNUAL`. Its year column is named `yr` (rather than `year`), and it does not include `peak_mw` or `hours_missing`. Use the SQL mart definition for the full set of fields.

docs/00_conventions.md:1: # 00. Conventions
docs/00_conventions.md:2: 
docs/00_conventions.md:3: > **At a glance:** Quick read: these are naming rules for files, SQL objects and charts. They are reference material, not part of the business conclusions.
docs/00_conventions.md:4: 
docs/00_conventions.md:5: Naming rules for the repository, the Snowflake objects, the Power BI model and git.
docs/00_conventions.md:6: 
docs/00_conventions.md:7: ## Repository and folders
docs/00_conventions.md:8: 
docs/00_conventions.md:9: - Repository: `dominion-reliability-review`, kebab-case.
docs/00_conventions.md:10: - Folders: lowercase, one word: `sql/`, `docs/`, `scripts/`, `data/`, `powerbi/`.
docs/00_conventions.md:11: - Under `data/`: `input/` (files as loaded), `reference/` (lookups), `output/` (derived tables), `raw/` (large downloads, ignored by git).
docs/00_conventions.md:12: 
docs/00_conventions.md:13: ## SQL files
docs/00_conventions.md:14: 
docs/00_conventions.md:15: - `NN_<content>.sql`, two-digit run order, snake_case.
docs/00_conventions.md:16: - One file covers one layer (01 raw and staging, 02 mart, 04 tests) or one source (05 NOAA, 06 EIA-930).
docs/00_conventions.md:17: - Analysis queries are in 03 or at the end of a source file. Grants are in 08.
docs/00_conventions.md:18: - Expected row counts and results are stated in a comment beside the statement that produces them.
docs/00_conventions.md:19: 
docs/00_conventions.md:20: ## Snowflake objects
docs/00_conventions.md:21: 
docs/00_conventions.md:22: Upper case, as Snowflake displays them.
docs/00_conventions.md:23: 
docs/00_conventions.md:24: | Object | Pattern | Examples |
docs/00_conventions.md:25: |---|---|---|
docs/00_conventions.md:26: | Database, schemas | `RELIABILITY`; `RAW`, `STG`, `MART` | |
docs/00_conventions.md:27: | File formats | `FF_<SOURCE>_CSV` | `FF_EIA_CSV`, `FF_NOAA_CSV`, `FF_EIA930_CSV` |
docs/00_conventions.md:28: | Stages | `<SOURCE>_STAGE` | `EIA_STAGE`, `PUDL_STAGE`, `NOAA_STAGE`, `EIA930_STAGE` |
docs/00_conventions.md:29: | RAW tables | `<SOURCE>_<TABLE>[_<YEAR>]`, one per source file, all columns VARCHAR | `EIA861_RELIABILITY_2025`, `PUDL_SALES`, `NOAA_STORM_EVENTS`, `EIA930_SUBREGION` |
docs/00_conventions.md:30: | STG views | entity name, no prefix, typed columns | `RELIABILITY`, `AMI`, `SALES`, `NOAA_VA_EVENTS`, `EIA930_DOM_HOURLY` |
docs/00_conventions.md:31: | MART | `DIM_` dimension, `FCT_` fact, `V_` view, plain noun for a derived table | `DIM_UTILITY`, `FCT_RELIABILITY`, `V_TREND`, `V_GROUP_INDEX`, `STORM_INDEX`, `DOM_LOAD_ANNUAL` |
docs/00_conventions.md:32: | Roles, users | `<PURPOSE>_READER`, `<TOOL>_SVC` | `BI_READER`, `POWERBI_SVC` |
docs/00_conventions.md:33: 
docs/00_conventions.md:34: ## Columns
docs/00_conventions.md:35: 
docs/00_conventions.md:36: snake_case.
docs/00_conventions.md:37: 
docs/00_conventions.md:38: | Suffix or word | Meaning | Examples |
docs/00_conventions.md:39: |---|---|---|
docs/00_conventions.md:40: | `_ex_med` | excluding major event days | `saidi_ex_med` |
docs/00_conventions.md:41: | `_all` | all events | `saidi_all` |
docs/00_conventions.md:42: | `_py` | prior year | `saidi_ex_med_py` |
docs/00_conventions.md:43: | `_5yr`, `_3yr` | rolling or period average | `saidi_ex_med_5yr` |
docs/00_conventions.md:44: | `cust_minutes_*`, `cust_interruptions_*` | additive numerators (SAIDI x customers, SAIFI x customers) | |
docs/00_conventions.md:45: | `_mw`, `_twh`, `_kv`, `_kt` | unit | `peak_mw`, `energy_twh`, `max_kv`, `max_gust_kt` |
docs/00_conventions.md:46: | `_pct` | percentage, already multiplied by 100 | `growth_pct` |
docs/00_conventions.md:47: | `_share` | fraction between 0 and 1 | `storm_share`, `ami_share` |
docs/00_conventions.md:48: | `_days` | count of county-days | `storm_county_days` |
docs/00_conventions.md:49: | `year` | data year, four digits | |
docs/00_conventions.md:50: | `*_key` | surrogate key built from IDs | `utility_key = utility_id \|\| '\|' \|\| state` |
docs/00_conventions.md:51: | `*_fips` | five-digit county FIPS as text with leading zeros | `county_id_fips` |
docs/00_conventions.md:52: 
docs/00_conventions.md:53: `customers` is a count of customers. `saidi_*` is minutes per customer. `adjusted` refers to the weather fit only. The major-event exclusion is always `_ex_med`.
docs/00_conventions.md:54: 
docs/00_conventions.md:55: ## Tests and analysis queries
docs/00_conventions.md:56: 
docs/00_conventions.md:57: - Tests: `T<n>`, numbered across files (T1 to T6 in 04, T7 to T9 in 05, T10 to T12 in 06). The expected result is stated in the comment above each test.
docs/00_conventions.md:58: - Analysis queries: `Q<n>`, with a letter suffix for variants (`Q3b`).
docs/00_conventions.md:59: 
docs/00_conventions.md:60: ## Documentation
docs/00_conventions.md:61: 
docs/00_conventions.md:62: - `NN_<topic>.md`, numbered in reading order.
docs/00_conventions.md:63: - Each document opens with one sentence stating its scope.
docs/00_conventions.md:64: - Figures quoted in documentation name the table and query they come from.
docs/00_conventions.md:65: 
docs/00_conventions.md:66: ## Data files
docs/00_conventions.md:67: 
docs/00_conventions.md:68: - Input files keep the publisher's file name (`Reliability_2025_States.csv`, `EIA930_SUBREGION_2025_Jan_Jun.csv`).
docs/00_conventions.md:69: - Output files are snake_case nouns (`storm_index.csv`, `dom_annual.csv`).
docs/00_conventions.md:70: - Files in `data/` are not edited by hand. Corrections are made in SQL.
docs/00_conventions.md:71: 
docs/00_conventions.md:72: ## Power BI
docs/00_conventions.md:73: 
docs/00_conventions.md:74: | Item | Convention | Examples |
docs/00_conventions.md:75: |---|---|---|
docs/00_conventions.md:76: | Measures | metric name, Title Case, scope in words | `SAIDI ex MED`, `SAIDI 3yr ending 2025`, `CAIDI 5yr` |
docs/00_conventions.md:77: | Helper measures | named for what they return | `Bar colour`, `SAIFI label`, `Hour colour` |
docs/00_conventions.md:78: | Calculated columns | Title Case | `Display name`, `Short name`, `Region` |
docs/00_conventions.md:79: | Columns from Snowflake | Snowflake names unchanged | `SAIDI_EX_MED` |
docs/00_conventions.md:80: | Visual titles | sentence case, state the finding | "Outages per customer up 6% since 2019, most of it after 2023" |
docs/00_conventions.md:81: | Visual subtitles | unit and source | "SAIFI ex MED, 2015 = 100. Dominion vs all Virginia co-ops. EIA-861" |
docs/00_conventions.md:82: | Report tabs | `Summary`, `Scorecard`, `What moved`, `Weather`, `Fair comparison`, `Growth`, `Data notes` | |
docs/00_conventions.md:83: | Colours | `#0A66C2` subject of the chart, `#B8BCC2` context, `#6B7280` second context, `#E8B117` third category, `#1F2A44` dark accent | |
docs/00_conventions.md:84: 
docs/00_conventions.md:85: ## Git
docs/00_conventions.md:86: 
docs/00_conventions.md:87: - Branch `main`.
docs/00_conventions.md:88: - Commit messages: imperative mood, under 72 characters, one logical change per commit.
docs/00_conventions.md:89: - Tags per presented version: `v1.0`, `v1.1`.
docs/00_conventions.md:90: - `CHANGELOG.md` entry per load: date, version, row counts, tests whose result changed.
docs/00_conventions.md:91: - Not committed: raw downloads over 25 MB, `.pbix` files, credentials.

docs/01_sources.md:55: ## How to re-download
docs/01_sources.md:56: 
docs/01_sources.md:57: ```bash
docs/01_sources.md:58: scripts/download_noaa.sh      # 13 files into data/raw/noaa/
docs/01_sources.md:59: scripts/download_eia930.sh    # 17 files into data/raw/eia930/
docs/01_sources.md:60: python scripts/extract_pudl.py   # rewrites data/input/pudl_*.csv from PUDL parquet
docs/01_sources.md:61: ```
docs/01_sources.md:62: 
docs/01_sources.md:63: EIA-861 2025 (S1, S7): download https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia861/zip/f8612025.zip, open `Reliability_2025.xlsx` and `Service_Territory_2025.xlsx` and save the named sheets as CSV. The Reliability sheet has three header rows and uses "." for blanks; file format `FF_EIA_CSV` handles both (`SKIP_HEADER = 3`, `NULL_IF = ('.')`).
docs/01_sources.md:64: 
docs/01_sources.md:65: ## Refresh cadence
docs/01_sources.md:66: 
docs/01_sources.md:67: | Source | Refresh cadence | What to re-run |
docs/01_sources.md:68: |---|---|---|
docs/01_sources.md:69: | EIA-861 | Annual, final data each October for the prior year | 01, 02, 04, then 03 |
docs/01_sources.md:70: | PUDL | Follows EIA releases, typically a few weeks after | `extract_pudl.py`, then 01, 02, 04 |
docs/01_sources.md:71: | NOAA Storm Events | Monthly, with a lag of about 90 days. Files are re-issued (new `_c` stamp) when events are revised | 05 |
docs/01_sources.md:72: | EIA-930 | Daily. Subregion data lags 1 to 30 days | 06, 07 |

docs/02_data_dictionary.md:1: # 02. Data dictionary
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:2: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:3: > **At a glance:** Quick read: RAW preserves imported records, STG standardizes them, and MART holds calculated metrics ready for analysis. For Dominion Virginia, the utility key is 19876|VA.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:4: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:5: Database `RELIABILITY`, three schemas.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:6: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:7: | Schema | Rule | Who writes | Who reads |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:8: |---|---|---|---|
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:9: | `RAW` | Every column `VARCHAR`. One table per source file, loaded by `COPY INTO`. Not modified after load. | load scripts | `STG` |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:10: | `STG` | Views. Typing (`TRY_TO_NUMBER`, `TRY_TO_DATE`), separator removal, unpivot, de-duplication. No business logic. | `01`, `05`, `06` | `MART` |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:11: | `MART` | Tables and views with the business logic: keys, peer groups, additive numerators, indexes, fits. | `02`, `05`, `06`, `07` | Power BI (`BI_READER`), analysts |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:12: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:13: Grain is stated for each object. A utility is identified by `utility_key = utility_id || '|' || state`. EIA reports one row per utility per state, so a utility operating in two states is two reporting units.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:14: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:15: ## RAW
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:16: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:17: ### `RAW.EIA861_RELIABILITY_2025` (977 rows, one per utility-state, 2025)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:18: Sheet `Reliability_States` of EIA's `Reliability_2025.xlsx`. The sheet has two blocks side by side: utilities that report under IEEE 1366 fill the `ieee_*` columns, utilities with their own major-event rule fill the `oth_*` columns.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:19: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:20: | Column | Meaning |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:21: |---|---|
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:22: | `data_year`, `utility_number`, `utility_name`, `state`, `ownership` | Identity. `ownership` is EIA's entity type (Investor Owned, Cooperative, Municipal, ...) |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:23: | `ieee_saidi_all`, `ieee_saifi_all`, `ieee_caidi_all` | All events, IEEE 1366 filers |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:24: | `ieee_saidi_ex_med`, `ieee_saifi_ex_med`, `ieee_caidi_ex_med` | Excluding major event days (IEEE 1366 2.5 beta method) |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:25: | `ieee_saidi_los`, `ieee_saifi_los`, `ieee_caidi_los` | Excluding loss-of-supply events |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:26: | `ieee_customers`, `ieee_max_kv`, `ieee_auto_recorded` | Customers served, highest distribution voltage, whether outages are logged automatically (OMS) |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:27: | `oth_saidi_all` ... `oth_caidi_ex_med` | Same six metrics for utilities using their own storm rule |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:28: | `oth_customers`, `oth_inactive_included`, `oth_momentary_def`, `oth_max_kv`, `oth_auto_recorded` | Same, plus the definition of a momentary interruption the utility applies |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:29: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:30: Blanks are ".", numbers carry thousands separators. Three header rows.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:31: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:32: ### `RAW.PUDL_RELIABILITY` (24,358 rows, utility-state-year-standard, 2013 to 2024)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:33: PUDL's cleaned long form of the same sheet across years. `standard` is `ieee_standard` or `other_standard`; both rows exist even when one is empty. `saidi_w_med` = with major event days (all events), `saidi_wo_med` = without. `data_maturity` is PUDL's flag (`final`, `provisional`).
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:34: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:35: ### `RAW.PUDL_AMI` (127,720 rows, utility-state-year-customer class)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:36: Meter counts: `advanced_metering_infrastructure` (smart meters), `automated_meter_reading` (one-way AMR), `non_amr_ami` (standard meters), `energy_served_ami_mwh`, `direct_load_control_customers`, `daily_digital_access_customers`, `home_area_network`.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:37: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:38: ### `RAW.PUDL_SALES` (198,770 rows, utility-state-year-class-service type)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:39: `sales_mwh`, `customers`, `sales_revenue` by `customer_class` (residential, commercial, industrial, transportation, total) and `service_type` (bundled, delivery, energy).
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:40: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:41: ### `RAW.NOAA_STORM_EVENTS` (831,288 rows, one per event, 2013 to 2025)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:42: 51 columns as published. Used: `state`, `year`, `event_type`, `cz_type` (`C` county, `Z` forecast zone), `cz_fips`, `cz_name`, `begin_yearmonth`, `begin_day`, `magnitude` (wind in knots for Thunderstorm Wind), `episode_id`, `event_id`.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:43: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:44: ### `RAW.NOAA_ZONE_COUNTY` (7,087 rows, zone-county)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:45: NWS lookup: `state`, `zone`, `county_name`, `fips`. A forecast zone can span several counties.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:46: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:47: ### `RAW.EIA861_SERVICE_TERRITORY_2025` (11,803 rows, utility-state-county)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:48: Which counties each utility serves. `utility_number`, `state`, `county`.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:49: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:50: ### `RAW.EIA930_SUBREGION` (6,077,232 rows, BA-subregion-hour, July 2018 to October 2026)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:51: `balancing_authority`, `sub_region`, `data_date`, `hour_number` (1 to 24, hour ending), `demand_mw`, `local_time_end`, `utc_time_end`.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:52: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:53: ## STG
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:54: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:55: | View | Grain | What it does |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:56: |---|---|---|
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:57: | `STG.RELIABILITY_2025` | utility-state-year (2025) | Unpivots the IEEE and Other blocks into one shape with a `standard` column. Types every metric as `NUMBER(12,3)`. |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:58: | `STG.RELIABILITY_HIST` | utility-state-year (2013 to 2024) | Types PUDL, maps `ieee_standard` -> `IEEE 1366`, `other_standard` -> `Other`. Drops rows with no SAIDI at all. |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:59: | `STG.RELIABILITY` | utility-state-year | Union of the two. Seven small utilities filed both standards in 2021: keep the IEEE row (`QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() ... = 1`) so customers are not double counted. |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:60: | `STG.AMI` | utility-state-year | Sums meter counts across classes, computes `ami_share`. |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:61: | `STG.SALES` | utility-state-year-class-service type | Typed sales, customers, revenue. |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:62: | `STG.DOMINION_VA_COUNTIES` | county | The 104 Virginia counties and cities Dominion serves, as 5-digit FIPS. EIA writes "Alexandria City", NOAA writes "City of Alexandria", both spellings are tried. |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:63: | `STG.NOAA_VA_EVENTS` | event x county | Virginia events with a county FIPS: county events directly, zone events through the zone lookup, then a second pass on county name for zones renumbered since the lookup was built. Adds `line_risk` (wind, winter, tropical, other). |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:64: | `STG.EIA930_DOM_HOURLY` | hour (72,864) | PJM / DOM only. `utc_ts` for the hour, `demand_mw_reported` as filed, `demand_mw` with values above 60,000 set to NULL. |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:65: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:66: Common typed columns in `STG.RELIABILITY`:

docs/02_data_dictionary.md:69: |---|---|---|---|
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:70: | `year` | NUMBER | | Data year |

docs/02_data_dictionary.md:83: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:84: ## MART
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:85: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:86: ### `MART.DIM_UTILITY` (one row per utility_key)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:87: `utility_id`, `state`, `utility_key`, `utility_name` (latest filed), `entity_type`, `peer_group`. Peer groups are assigned by ID rather than by name and are listed in `docs/04_decisions.md` D10:
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:88: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:89: | peer_group | Members |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:90: |---|---|
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:91: | Dominion Energy Virginia | 19876-VA |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:92: | Virginia co-op | every Virginia utility with entity type Cooperative |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:93: | Appalachian Power (VA) | 733-VA, kept separate because its mountain terrain makes it an outlier |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:94: | Southeast IOU peer | Duke Carolinas 5416-NC, Duke Progress 3046-NC, Georgia Power 7140-GA, Alabama Power 195-AL, Dominion SC 17539-SC |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:95: | Mid-Atlantic IOU peer | BGE 1167-MD, Pepco 15270-MD, PECO 14940-PA, PPL 14715-PA |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:96: | Florida IOU peer | FPL 6452-FL, Duke Energy Florida 6455-FL |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:97: | Other | all remaining reporting units (about 950) |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:98: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:99: ### `MART.FCT_RELIABILITY` (13,095 rows, utility_key x year, 2013 to 2025)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:100: All columns of `STG.RELIABILITY` plus the additive numerators used for aggregation:
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:101: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:102: | Column | Formula | Purpose |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:103: |---|---|---|
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:104: | `storm_saidi`, `storm_saifi` | all - ex_med | Minutes and interruptions attributable to major event days |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:105: | `cust_minutes_all`, `cust_minutes_ex_med` | SAIDI x customers | Total customer-minutes. Summed across utilities or years and divided by summed customers. |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:106: | `cust_interruptions_all`, `cust_interruptions_ex_med` | SAIFI x customers | Total customer-interruptions, aggregated the same way |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:107: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:108: ### `MART.V_TREND` (view, utility_key x year)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:109: Adds prior-year values (`*_py`), `saidi_yoy`, `storm_share`, five-year rolling averages and `frequency_share_of_change` = ln(SAIFI ratio) / ln(SAIDI ratio), the share of a SAIDI change attributable to frequency. Arguments to `LN` are guarded with `NULLIF` because Snowflake raises an error on `LN(0)` and some utilities report zero.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:110: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:111: ### `MART.V_GROUP_INDEX` (view, peer_group x year)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:112: Customer-weighted SAIFI, SAIDI, CAIDI for each peer group: `SUM(cust_interruptions_ex_med) / SUM(customers of utilities that reported it)`. Used for the co-op benchmark. See `docs/03` P18 on the denominator.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:113: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:114: ### `MART.V_VA_COOP_PANEL` (view, created in `03_analysis.sql`)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:115: Balanced panel of Virginia co-ops with at least 10,000 customers that filed in every year 2015 to 2025 (seven utilities). Used in Q3 as a robustness check. The report uses the all-co-op group from `V_GROUP_INDEX`.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:116: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:117: ### `MART.STORM_INDEX` (13 rows, year)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:118: | Column | Meaning |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:119: |---|---|
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:120: | `storm_county_days` | Count of (county, date) pairs in Dominion's Virginia counties with at least one wind, winter or tropical event. Multiple reports in one county on one day count once. |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:121: | `wind_days`, `winter_days`, `tropical_days` | Same, by event family |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:122: | `severe_gust_days` | County-days with a Thunderstorm Wind gust of 60 knots or more |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:123: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:124: ### `MART.V_STORM_VS_SAIDI` (view, year)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:125: `STORM_INDEX` joined to Dominion's rows in `FCT_RELIABILITY`: adds `saidi_ex_med`, `saifi_ex_med`, `storm_saidi`. Source for the scatter chart and the weather fit.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:126: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:127: ### `MART.SAIDI_WEATHER_ADJUSTED` (11 rows, year 2015 to 2025)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:128: | Column | Meaning |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:129: |---|---|
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:130: | `predicted_from_weather` | intercept + slope x storm_county_days, fitted on 2015 to 2024 (intercept 104.4, slope 0.0514 minutes per county-day) |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:131: | `weather_adjusted_saidi` | actual - predicted + mean(2015 to 2024). The year's SAIDI at average weather exposure |
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:132: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:133: ### `MART.DOM_LOAD_ANNUAL` (9 rows, year 2018 to 2026)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:134: `winter_peak_mw` (Dec, Jan, Feb of the calendar year), `summer_peak_mw` (Jun to Aug), `peak_mw`, `floor_mw` (lowest hour), `median_mw`, `avg_mw`, `energy_twh`, `hours_reported`, `hours_missing`. 2018 is July onward and 2026 is year to date.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:135: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:136: ### `MART.DOM_TOP50_HOURS` (96 rows)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:137: The fifty highest-demand hours ranked at two reference dates: `rank_end_2025` (record to 31 December 2025, comparable with the annual report) and `rank_today`. A row is included if it is in either top fifty.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:138: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:139: ### `MART.DOM_LOAD_PROFILE` (24 rows, hour of day)
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:140: `avg_mw_2019`, `avg_mw_2025`, `growth_pct` for each hour ending 1 to 24.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:141: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:142: ## Naming conventions
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:143: 
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:144: - Tables and views upper case, columns lower case snake case, as Snowflake displays them.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:145: - `V_` prefix for views in MART, none in STG (everything in STG is a view).
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:146: - `_ex_med` = excluding major event days, `_all` = all events. `adjusted` refers only to the weather fit.
docs/02_data_dictionary.md:147: - `customers` is a count of customers. `saidi_*` is minutes per customer.
docs/03_data_quality.md:1: # 03. Data quality
docs/03_data_quality.md:2: 
docs/03_data_quality.md:3: > **At a glance:** Quick read: these checks prevent misleading conclusions caused by duplicate utilities, inconsistent formatting, mis-mapped counties and implausible demand readings. A successful check does not prove the utility filing itself was correct.
docs/03_data_quality.md:4: 
docs/03_data_quality.md:5: Profiling findings on the raw files, the handling of each in SQL and the tests T1 to T12 that run after each load.
docs/03_data_quality.md:6: 
docs/03_data_quality.md:7: ## Profiling findings
docs/03_data_quality.md:8: 
docs/03_data_quality.md:9: Found by profiling RAW (`sql/00_look_first.sql`) or by a test on the first load. Each is handled in SQL so that it applies to every subsequent load.
docs/03_data_quality.md:10: 
docs/03_data_quality.md:11: | # | Source | Finding | Handling | Location |
docs/03_data_quality.md:12: |---|---|---|---|---|
docs/03_data_quality.md:13: | P1 | EIA-861 2025 | Three header rows; blanks written as "." | File format `SKIP_HEADER = 3`, `NULL_IF = ('.')` | `01`, `FF_EIA_CSV` |
docs/03_data_quality.md:14: | P2 | EIA-861 2025 | Numbers carry thousands separators ("2,675,735") | `TRY_TO_NUMBER(REPLACE(x, ',', ''))` on every numeric column | `01`, `STG.RELIABILITY_2025` |
docs/03_data_quality.md:15: | P3 | EIA-861 2025 | IEEE 1366 and Other-standard metrics in two side-by-side column blocks; each utility fills one | Unpivot with `UNION ALL`; `standard` column records the block | `01` |
docs/03_data_quality.md:16: | P4 | EIA-861 2025 | Eight utilities report ex-MED values without all-events values | Retained: filter is `saidi_all IS NOT NULL OR saidi_ex_med IS NOT NULL` | `01` |
docs/03_data_quality.md:17: | P5 | PUDL | A row exists for both standards for every utility, including empty rows | Rows with no SAIDI under either standard are dropped | `01`, `STG.RELIABILITY_HIST` |
docs/03_data_quality.md:18: | P6 | PUDL and EIA union | Seven utilities filed under both standards in 2021 | De-duplicated with `QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() ... = 1`; the IEEE row is kept so customers are counted once | `01`, `STG.RELIABILITY` |
docs/03_data_quality.md:19: | P7 | EIA-861 | Counting rule changes in 2015 (automated outage recording) and 2020 (46 kV lines included) shift Dominion's series | Not adjusted in data. Marked on trend charts. 2013 and 2014 excluded from the weather fit | charts, `07` |
docs/03_data_quality.md:20: | P8 | EIA-861 | Four utility-years where all-events SAIDI is lower than ex-MED SAIDI | Retained in `FCT_RELIABILITY` so the load matches EIA. The utilities concerned are in the `Other` peer group and outside the peer sets. T3 reports the count on every run | `04`, T3 |
docs/03_data_quality.md:21: | P9 | NOAA | Zone events (`cz_type = 'Z'`) reference a forecast zone, not a county | Mapped to counties through the NWS zone-county correlation file | `05`, `STG.NOAA_VA_EVENTS` |
docs/03_data_quality.md:22: | P10 | NOAA | 140 zone events reference zones renumbered after the lookup was published | Second pass matches `UPPER(cz_name)` to the county name. T9 confirms none remain unmapped | `05`, T9 |
docs/03_data_quality.md:23: | P11 | NOAA vs EIA | County name spellings differ between sources ("Alexandria City" vs "City of Alexandria"); James City and Charles City are county names | Both spellings are tried; `MIN(fips)` per EIA county. T7 confirms all 104 counties resolve | `05`, T7 |
docs/03_data_quality.md:24: | P12 | NOAA | One storm can produce many event rows in one county on one day | The index counts county-days, not events | `05`, `STORM_INDEX` |
docs/03_data_quality.md:25: | P13 | EIA-930 | Two hours on 18 October 2021 report 1.5 billion and 1.0 billion MW for a zone that peaks near 25,000 MW | Values above 60,000 MW set to NULL in staging; the reported value is retained in `demand_mw_reported`. T10 lists the rows | `06`, T10 |
docs/03_data_quality.md:26: | P14 | EIA-930 | 2,020 hours have no demand value (1,729 in 2018) | Left NULL. `hours_missing` recorded per year in `DOM_LOAD_ANNUAL`. T12 checks each full year has at least 8,700 reported hours | `06`, T12 |
docs/03_data_quality.md:27: | P15 | EIA-930 | Local time has 23-hour and 25-hour days at clock changes | Hourly logic keyed on `utc_ts`. T11 confirms no duplicate hour | `06`, T11 |
docs/03_data_quality.md:28: | P16 | EIA-930 | `EIA930_SUBREGION_2018_Jan_Jun.csv` is served as an HTML error page (subregion reporting starts July 2018) | Not loaded. The download script removes it | `scripts/download_eia930.sh` |
docs/03_data_quality.md:29: | P17 | EIA-861 vs annual report | EIA customers for Dominion VA are 2,675,735 and the annual report states 2,809k | Not an error. The report figure includes North Carolina. Stated on the scorecard | report |
docs/03_data_quality.md:30: | P18 | EIA-861 | Prince George Electric filed SAIDI but no SAIFI for 2025. A group denominator summing all members' customers would include its 13,341 customers and understate the co-op SAIFI (1.849 instead of 1.887) | `V_GROUP_INDEX` denominators sum only the customers of utilities that reported the numerator. The co-op index (each year relative to 2015) is unchanged to one decimal (137.4) | `02`, `V_GROUP_INDEX` |
docs/03_data_quality.md:31: | P19 | EIA-930 vs company reports | Dominion's earnings decks report DOM zone peaks of 22,189 MW (24 Dec 2022), 21,993 MW (28 Jul 2023) and 23,127 MW (16 Jul 2024). EIA-930 hourly maxima on the same days are 22,415, 22,050 and 23,220 MW, within 1.1% | Confirms EIA-930 as a proxy for the zone load. The difference is hourly-integrated EIA data against PJM's reported peak | `DOM_LOAD_ANNUAL`, `data/reference/dominion_ir_series.csv` |
docs/03_data_quality.md:32: 
docs/03_data_quality.md:33: ## Tests
docs/03_data_quality.md:34: 
docs/03_data_quality.md:35: Run after every load. A result other than the expected one stops the refresh until explained.
docs/03_data_quality.md:36: 
docs/03_data_quality.md:37: | Test | File | Check | Expected | Result, this build | If it fails |
docs/03_data_quality.md:38: |---|---|---|---|---|---|
docs/03_data_quality.md:39: | T1 | 04 | No duplicate utility-state-year in `FCT_RELIABILITY` | 0 rows | 0 | De-duplication in `STG.RELIABILITY` failed, or a new file overlaps years already loaded |
docs/03_data_quality.md:40: | T2 | 04 | SAIDI = SAIFI x CAIDI within 2% for every row | 0 rows | 0 | A column is mis-mapped in staging |
docs/03_data_quality.md:41: | T3 | 04 | All-events SAIDI not lower than ex-MED SAIDI | 4 rows (known source anomalies, P8) | 4 | A different count means new anomalous rows. Investigate before using peer figures |
docs/03_data_quality.md:42: | T4 | 04 | Dominion VA 2025 equals EIA's published values (306.7, 144.4, 2,675,735) | 0 rows | 0 | Wrong file or wrong column mapping. Stop the refresh |
docs/03_data_quality.md:43: | T5 | 04 | Row count and customer total by year | 13 rows, 940 to 1,175 reporting units per year, customers increasing each year. The step from 1,175 (2019) to 941 (2020) reflects a change in which filers appear in the reliability table | 13 | A year with materially fewer rows indicates a parse failure |
docs/03_data_quality.md:44: | T6 | 04 | No raw numeric value failed `TRY_TO_NUMBER` after separator removal | 0 rows | 0 | A new formatting variant in the source. Extend the `REPLACE` |
docs/03_data_quality.md:45: | T7 | 05 | Every Dominion county in the service territory resolves to a FIPS code | 0 rows | 0 | A county spelling changed. Extend the name mapping |
docs/03_data_quality.md:46: | T8 | 05 | The latest NOAA file covers a full year | 1 row, month 12 | 202512 | The NOAA file is partial. Wait, or state the cut-off on the chart |
docs/03_data_quality.md:47: | T9 | 05 | No zone event left without a county after both passes | 0 rows | 0 | Further zone renumbering. Refresh the zone lookup |
docs/03_data_quality.md:48: | T10 | 06 | Hours with reported demand above 60,000 MW | 2 rows (18 October 2021) | 2 | Additional anomalous rows. Review the dates |
docs/03_data_quality.md:49: | T11 | 06 | No UTC hour appears twice for the DOM zone | 0 rows | 0 | A file was loaded twice. Truncate and reload |
docs/03_data_quality.md:50: | T12 | 06 | Each full year 2019 to 2025 has at least 8,700 reported hours | 0 rows | 0 | A six-month file is missing or truncated |
docs/03_data_quality.md:51: 
docs/03_data_quality.md:52: ### Form of the tests
docs/03_data_quality.md:53: 
docs/03_data_quality.md:54: Each test is a `SELECT` that returns the expected row count when the data is sound. Any user with read access can run them. In Snowflake they can be scheduled as a `TASK` that writes results to a table the report reads (see `docs/07_governance_and_runbook.md`). The same checks translate directly to dbt tests.
docs/03_data_quality.md:55: 
docs/03_data_quality.md:56: ### Not covered by the tests
docs/03_data_quality.md:57: 
docs/03_data_quality.md:58: - The accuracy of a utility's own filing. T2 checks internal consistency, not the reported customer count. EIA does not audit filings.
docs/03_data_quality.md:59: - Whether Dominion's own major-event rule excludes more or fewer days than IEEE 1366 would. Not testable from public data. Stated as a limitation on each peer chart.
docs/03_data_quality.md:60: - Outage causes, circuit-level detail and repeat-outage customers. Not available in any public source.
docs/04_decisions.md:1: # 04. Decisions
docs/04_decisions.md:2: 
docs/04_decisions.md:3: > **At a glance:** Quick read: I chose methods that support fairer comparisons, kept 2025 out of the weather model's 2015-2024 training set, and documented the remaining limitations. This is exploratory analysis, not causal attribution.
docs/04_decisions.md:4: 
docs/04_decisions.md:5: Design decisions, each with context, the decision, alternatives considered and consequences.
docs/04_decisions.md:6: 
docs/04_decisions.md:7: ---
docs/04_decisions.md:8: 
docs/04_decisions.md:9: ### D1. Aggregation uses additive numerators
docs/04_decisions.md:10: 
docs/04_decisions.md:11: **Context.** SAIDI, SAIFI and CAIDI are ratios published per utility. Comparisons across utilities or years require an aggregate.
docs/04_decisions.md:12: 
docs/04_decisions.md:13: **Decision.** `FCT_RELIABILITY` stores `cust_minutes_ex_med = saidi_ex_med x customers` and `cust_interruptions_ex_med = saifi_ex_med x customers`. Aggregates sum these and divide by summed customers. In DAX: `DIVIDE(SUM(minutes), SUM(customers))`.
docs/04_decisions.md:14: 
docs/04_decisions.md:15: **Alternatives.** `AVG(saidi)` across utilities. Rejected: it weights every utility equally regardless of size. For the Virginia co-ops in 2025 the simple average SAIFI is 2.44 and the customer-weighted figure is 1.89.
docs/04_decisions.md:16: 
docs/04_decisions.md:17: **Consequence.** Multi-year averages are averages of annual customer-weighted values (`AVERAGEX(VALUES(YEAR), [SAIDI ex MED])`), which matches Dominion's definition of its three-year figure and reproduces 133.0.
docs/04_decisions.md:18: 
docs/04_decisions.md:19: ---
docs/04_decisions.md:20: 
docs/04_decisions.md:21: ### D2. Ex-MED is the headline measure, with all-events shown alongside
docs/04_decisions.md:22: 
docs/04_decisions.md:23: **Context.** EIA publishes both. Dominion reports the ex-MED figure to the SCC and in its annual report.
docs/04_decisions.md:24: 
docs/04_decisions.md:25: **Decision.** Trends and comparisons use ex-MED. All-events appears on the scorecard and in the stacked bar chart so that the storm share is visible.
docs/04_decisions.md:26: 
docs/04_decisions.md:27: **Alternatives.** All-events as the headline. Rejected: the series is dominated by major storms and does not describe day-to-day performance.
docs/04_decisions.md:28: 
docs/04_decisions.md:29: **Consequence.** Dominion files under its own major-event rule (EIA "Other" standard). Most large peers file under IEEE 1366. The two are not reconciled by any public source. Each peer chart carries this caveat and the peer table shows the method per utility.
docs/04_decisions.md:30: 
docs/04_decisions.md:31: ---
docs/04_decisions.md:32: 
docs/04_decisions.md:33: ### D3. RAW tables are all VARCHAR
docs/04_decisions.md:34: 
docs/04_decisions.md:35: **Context.** Source files contain "." for blanks, thousands separators, mixed-case booleans and multiple header rows.
docs/04_decisions.md:36: 
docs/04_decisions.md:37: **Decision.** `COPY INTO` tables in which every column is `VARCHAR`. Typing is done in STG views with `TRY_*` functions; a value that fails typing becomes NULL and T6 reports it.
docs/04_decisions.md:38: 
docs/04_decisions.md:39: **Alternatives.** Typed RAW tables with `ON_ERROR = CONTINUE`. Rejected: rows are dropped without a record.
docs/04_decisions.md:40: 
docs/04_decisions.md:41: **Consequence.** RAW is an exact copy of the source. Typing rules can be changed without reloading.
docs/04_decisions.md:42: 
docs/04_decisions.md:43: ---
docs/04_decisions.md:44: 
docs/04_decisions.md:45: ### D4. Weather exposure is measured in county-days
docs/04_decisions.md:46: 
docs/04_decisions.md:47: **Context.** NOAA records one row per event. A single storm can produce many rows in one county on one day.
docs/04_decisions.md:48: 
docs/04_decisions.md:49: **Decision.** `STORM_INDEX` counts distinct (county, date) pairs with at least one line-risk event. 796 county-days in 2025.
docs/04_decisions.md:50: 
docs/04_decisions.md:51: **Alternatives.** Event counts (over-weight well-reported storms). Damage estimates (sparse and inconsistent). Heating and cooling degree days (temperature only and already quoted in the annual report).
docs/04_decisions.md:52: 
docs/04_decisions.md:53: **Consequence.** The index correlates 0.70 with SAIDI ex-MED over 2015 to 2024 and 0.41 with SAIFI. It measures exposure, not damage.
docs/04_decisions.md:54: 
docs/04_decisions.md:55: ---
docs/04_decisions.md:56: 
docs/04_decisions.md:57: ### D5. Event types classified as line risk
docs/04_decisions.md:58: 
docs/04_decisions.md:59: **Decision.** Wind: Thunderstorm Wind, High Wind, Strong Wind, Tornado. Winter: Ice Storm, Winter Storm, Heavy Snow, Blizzard, Sleet. Tropical: Hurricane, Hurricane (Typhoon), Tropical Storm, Tropical Depression. All other types (flood, heat, hail, lightning, drought) are classified `other` and excluded.
docs/04_decisions.md:60: 
docs/04_decisions.md:61: **Alternatives.** Include Lightning and Hail. Rejected: lightning reporting is inconsistent across years and hail rarely causes line outages. Include Flash Flood. Rejected: flood damage affects substations and appears in major event days, which ex-MED already excludes.
docs/04_decisions.md:62: 
docs/04_decisions.md:63: **Consequence.** The classification is one `CASE` expression in `STG.NOAA_VA_EVENTS`.
docs/04_decisions.md:64: 
docs/04_decisions.md:65: ---
docs/04_decisions.md:66: 
docs/04_decisions.md:67: ### D6. Weather fit window is 2015 to 2024
docs/04_decisions.md:68: 
docs/04_decisions.md:69: **Context.** `SAIDI_WEATHER_ADJUSTED` requires a fitted relationship between storm county-days and SAIDI.
docs/04_decisions.md:70: 
docs/04_decisions.md:71: **Decision.** Fit on 2015 to 2024 and predict 2025 out of sample.
docs/04_decisions.md:72: 
docs/04_decisions.md:73: **Alternatives.** Include 2013 and 2014. Rejected: the 2015 change to automated outage recording shifts the level. Include 2025. Rejected: 2025 is the year under test.
docs/04_decisions.md:74: 
docs/04_decisions.md:75: **Consequence.** Ten observations. Intercept 104.4, slope 0.051 minutes per county-day. The fit is a check on one year, not a forecasting model.
docs/04_decisions.md:76: 
docs/04_decisions.md:77: ---
docs/04_decisions.md:78: 
docs/04_decisions.md:79: ### D7. Hourly demand above 60,000 MW is treated as a reporting error
docs/04_decisions.md:80: 
docs/04_decisions.md:81: **Context.** Two hours in October 2021 report over one billion MW for the DOM zone.
docs/04_decisions.md:82: 
docs/04_decisions.md:83: **Decision.** Values above 60,000 MW are set to NULL in `STG.EIA930_DOM_HOURLY`. The DOM zone has not exceeded 26,000 MW; PJM as a whole peaks near 150,000 MW. Reported values are retained in `demand_mw_reported`.
docs/04_decisions.md:84: 
docs/04_decisions.md:85: **Alternatives.** Replace with the mean of neighbouring hours. Rejected: fabricates data. Drop the rows. Rejected: removes the record of the anomaly.
docs/04_decisions.md:86: 
docs/04_decisions.md:87: ---
docs/04_decisions.md:88: 
docs/04_decisions.md:89: ### D8. Hourly data is keyed on UTC
docs/04_decisions.md:90: 
docs/04_decisions.md:91: **Decision.** `utc_ts` is the key for all hourly operations. Local time is retained for display.
docs/04_decisions.md:92: 
docs/04_decisions.md:93: **Reason.** Local time has 23-hour and 25-hour days at clock changes. T11 confirms no duplicate hours.
docs/04_decisions.md:94: 
docs/04_decisions.md:95: ---
docs/04_decisions.md:96: 
docs/04_decisions.md:97: ### D9. Top-50 hours are ranked at two reference dates
docs/04_decisions.md:98: 
docs/04_decisions.md:99: **Context.** Dominion's annual report states that 38 of the 50 highest peak loads in DOM Zone history occurred after 1 January 2025. The statement has a reference date.
docs/04_decisions.md:100: 
docs/04_decisions.md:101: **Decision.** `DOM_TOP50_HOURS` ranks hours as of 31 December 2025 (comparable with the report) and as of the load date. The report card uses the first.
docs/04_decisions.md:102: 
docs/04_decisions.md:103: **Consequence.** 47 of 50 as of end 2025 and 46 of 50 as of October 2026. The EIA record starts in July 2018, so earlier years present in Dominion's own ranking are absent, which accounts for 47 exceeding 38.
docs/04_decisions.md:104: 
docs/04_decisions.md:105: ---
docs/04_decisions.md:106: 
docs/04_decisions.md:107: ### D10. Peer set
docs/04_decisions.md:108: 
docs/04_decisions.md:109: **Decision.** Twelve large investor-owned utilities in PJM and the Southeast, selected by EIA ID before results were computed. Southeast: Duke Carolinas, Duke Progress, Georgia Power, Alabama Power, Dominion SC. Mid-Atlantic (PJM): BGE, Pepco, PECO, PPL. Florida: FPL, Duke Energy Florida. Plus Dominion VA. Appalachian Power (VA) is held in a separate group and shown separately because its SAIDI exceeds 300 minutes and would compress the scale of every chart.
docs/04_decisions.md:110: 
docs/04_decisions.md:111: **Alternatives.** All investor-owned utilities with over one million customers nationally. Rejected: differences in climate and vegetation across regions would require a weather model outside the scope of this project.
docs/04_decisions.md:112: 
docs/04_decisions.md:113: **Consequence.** Rankings are statements about this set only. The peer table lists every member and its reporting method.
docs/04_decisions.md:114: 
docs/04_decisions.md:115: ---
docs/04_decisions.md:116: 
docs/04_decisions.md:117: ### D11. Two co-op benchmarks
docs/04_decisions.md:118: 
docs/04_decisions.md:119: **Decision.** `V_GROUP_INDEX` uses all Virginia cooperatives, customer-weighted; this is the series on the report. `V_VA_COOP_PANEL` is a balanced panel of the seven co-ops with at least 10,000 customers that filed in every year 2015 to 2025, used in `03_analysis.sql` Q3 to check that the result is not driven by entry and exit. Q3d repeats the comparison leaving each co-op out in turn.
docs/04_decisions.md:120: 
docs/04_decisions.md:121: **Consequence.** Both benchmarks give the same direction. The report states "all Virginia co-ops combined".
docs/04_decisions.md:122: 
docs/04_decisions.md:123: ---
docs/04_decisions.md:124: 
docs/04_decisions.md:125: ### D12. Peer groups are assigned by EIA utility ID
docs/04_decisions.md:126: 
docs/04_decisions.md:127: **Reason.** Utility names change (Virginia Electric & Power Co trades as Dominion Energy Virginia); IDs do not. `DIM_UTILITY.peer_group` is a `CASE` on `(utility_id, state)`.
docs/04_decisions.md:128: 
docs/04_decisions.md:129: ---
docs/04_decisions.md:130: 
docs/04_decisions.md:131: ### D13. DirectQuery during development, Import for presentation
docs/04_decisions.md:132: 
docs/04_decisions.md:133: **Decision.** The report was developed in DirectQuery against Snowflake. For presentation it is switched to Import so that it opens without a network connection or a running warehouse. The DirectQuery copy is retained separately.
docs/04_decisions.md:134: 
docs/04_decisions.md:135: **Consequence.** The Import file carries a snapshot date on its title page.
docs/04_decisions.md:136: 
docs/04_decisions.md:137: ---
docs/04_decisions.md:138: 
docs/04_decisions.md:139: ### D14. Scope limits
docs/04_decisions.md:140: 
docs/04_decisions.md:141: - This public-data project does not estimate the effect of the grid transformation programme. The weather-adjusted SAIDI series is a model-based comparison, not evidence of the programme's causal benefit; feeder-level project data would be required for that assessment.
docs/04_decisions.md:142: - The project does not rank Dominion nationally. It places Dominion within a defined peer set with reporting methods stated.
docs/04_decisions.md:143: - The project does not attribute load growth to data centres from the data. EIA-861 does not identify customers. It reports the shape of the growth (night hours rising faster than day hours and commercial sales rising far faster than commercial customer counts), which is consistent with round-the-clock load.
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:1: # 05. Metrics and measures
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:2: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:3: > **At a glance:** Quick read: SAIDI is outage minutes per customer, SAIFI is interruptions per customer, and CAIDI is average minutes per customer-interruption. SAIDI = SAIFI x CAIDI. The recent increase is more frequency-driven.
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:4: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:5: Definitions of the reliability indices and the calculation behind each figure on the report. DAX for all measures is in `powerbi/measures.dax`.
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:6: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:7: ## Reliability indices
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:8: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:9: Each index is derived from two annual totals reported by the utility, customer-interruptions and customer-minutes of interruption, divided by customers served.
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:10: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:11: | Index | Name used on the report | Formula | Unit | Measures |
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:12: |---|---|---|---|---|
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:13: | SAIFI | Outages per customer | customer-interruptions / customers | interruptions per customer per year | Frequency of interruption |
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:14: | SAIDI | Outage minutes per customer | customer-minutes / customers | minutes per customer per year | Total interruption time per customer |
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:15: | CAIDI | Minutes to restore each outage | SAIDI / SAIFI | minutes per interruption | Average duration of an interruption |
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:16: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:17: SAIDI = SAIFI x CAIDI by definition (test T2 checks the identity in the data). A change in SAIDI therefore decomposes into a frequency component and a duration component:
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:18: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:19: ln(SAIDI2 / SAIDI1) = ln(SAIFI2 / SAIFI1) + ln(CAIDI2 / CAIDI1)
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:20: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:21: The share of a SAIDI change attributable to frequency is ln(SAIFI ratio) / ln(SAIDI ratio). This is `V_TREND.frequency_share_of_change` and the `Frequency share` measures.
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:22: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:23: ## Major event days
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:24: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:25: Each index is reported in two forms:
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:26: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:27: - All events.
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:28: - Excluding major event days (ex-MED): days classified as major events are removed. Regulatory reporting and the annual report use this form.
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:29: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:30: IEEE Standard 1366 defines a major event day statistically (a day whose SAIDI exceeds a threshold derived from the previous five years, the 2.5 beta method). Dominion, Alabama Power and some other utilities apply their own definition and file under EIA's "Other" standard. The report shows the method beside each peer figure.
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:31: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:32: **Major-event-day exclusion share** = (SAIDI all - SAIDI ex-MED) / SAIDI all. The existing SQL/DAX field names `storm_share` and `storm_saidi` are legacy labels: this difference is **not** an independent NOAA attribution of storm-caused outage minutes. For Dominion in 2025: (306.7 - 144.4) / 306.7 = 53%.
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:33: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:34: ## Aggregation rule
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:35: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:36: Ratios are not averaged. Numerators and denominators are summed separately and divided once.
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:37: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:38: | Not used | Used |
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:39: |---|---|
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:40: | `AVERAGE(saidi_ex_med)` across utilities | `SUM(cust_minutes_ex_med) / SUM(customers)` |
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:41: | `AVERAGE(saidi_ex_med)` across years for one utility | `AVERAGEX(VALUES(year), [SAIDI ex MED])`: the customer-weighted value for each year, then the mean of the years |
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:42: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:43: ## Report figures
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:44: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:45: ### Scorecard
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:46: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:47: | Card | Value | Measure | Calculation |
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:48: |---|---|---|---|
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:49: | Outage minutes per customer, 3-year average 2023 to 2025 | 133.0 | `SAIDI 3yr ending 2025` | Customer-weighted SAIDI ex-MED for each of 2023, 2024, 2025, then the mean. Annual report page 10: 133 |
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:50: | 3-year average 2022 to 2024 | 130.2 | `SAIDI 3yr ending 2024` | Same for 2022 to 2024. Report: 130 |
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:51: | Customers served, 2025 | 2,675,735 | `Total Customers` filtered to 2025 | EIA `oth_customers`. Virginia only. The annual report's 2,809k includes North Carolina |
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:52: | Outages per customer, 2025 | 1.32 | `SAIFI ex MED` | 2025 row |
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:53: | Minutes to restore each outage, 2025 | 109.4 | `CAIDI ex MED` | SAIDI / SAIFI |
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:54: | Including major storms, 2025 | 306.7 | `SAIDI all events` | 2025 row |
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:55: | Storm share, 2025 | 53% | `Storm share` | (306.7 - 144.4) / 306.7 |

docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:107: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:108: ## Measure naming
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:109: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:110: Measures use the metric name (`SAIDI ex MED`, `CAIDI 5yr`). Plain-language names are used in visual titles. Columns keep their Snowflake names, so each card can be traced to a column in `docs/02_data_dictionary.md`.
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:111: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:112: Formatting measures (`Bar colour`, `Dot colour`, `Row colour`, `SAIFI label`, `CAIDI label`, `Index label`, `Adjusted label`, `Hour colour`, `Change colour`) return colour strings or conditional labels and are listed at the end of `powerbi/measures.dax`.
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:113: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:114: ## Weather-model interpretation
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:115: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:116: The regression was fitted on only ten annual observations (2015-2024); the correlation shows association, not causation. The unusually small 2025 prediction error should not be presented as evidence of consistent forecasting accuracy. Annual NOAA storm county-days and ex-MED SAIDI also differ in which event days they cover. Weather-adjusted SAIDI is a model-based comparison, not an audited reliability measure.
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:117: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:118: ## Interpreting year-to-year attribution
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:119: 
docs/05_metrics_and_measures.md:120: When annual SAIDI barely changes, dividing the logarithmic SAIFI change by the tiny logarithmic SAIDI change gives an unstable percentage. In `V_TREND`, the frequency-share calculation is now left blank when the absolute logarithmic SAIDI change is below 0.02 (approximately a 2% annual change). The longer 2023-to-2025 comparison remains available and is interpretable. Suppressing a ratio is not suppressing the actual SAIDI/SAIFI/CAIDI values.
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:1: # 06. Power BI report
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:2: 
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:3: > **At a glance:** Quick read: start with frequency versus restoration duration, then view weather as context and hourly demand as a separate growth story. Some existing Power BI chart titles may require manual updates in Desktop.
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:4: 
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:5: Report file: `Dominion_Reliability.pbix` (not committed; see `docs/07_governance_and_runbook.md`). Model: ten objects from `RELIABILITY.MART` and three workbook sheets. One relationship: `FCT_RELIABILITY[UTILITY_KEY]` many-to-one `DIM_UTILITY[UTILITY_KEY]`. All other tables are keyed by year or hour and have no relationships.
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:6: 
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:7: ## Design rules
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:8: 
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:9: | Rule | Reason |
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:10: |---|---|
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:11: | Title states the finding. Subtitle states unit and source | The titles summarise the report on their own |
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:12: | `#0A66C2` for the subject of the chart (Dominion, or the series the title refers to); `#B8BCC2` for context; `#E8B117` only where a third category is required (day hours, summer) | Consistent colour meaning across pages |
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:13: | Straight lines between annual points, markers shown | Smoothed lines imply values between the annual observations |
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:14: | Counting changes (2015, 2020) marked as dashed vertical lines on each reliability trend | Separates changes in measurement from changes in performance |
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:15: | Reference line where an anchor is needed (2019 level, 2015 = 100, ten-year average, peer medians) | Makes non-zero axes readable |
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:16: | Data labels on the points referred to in the title | Reduces clutter |
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:17: | All figures come from measures, not dragged columns | No "Sum of" headers. Aggregation is customer-weighted |
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:18: | One headline text box per page, same position and size | Consistent layout |
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:19:

docs/06_powerbi_report.md:52: Headline: *What was checked and what public data cannot show.*

The additional NOAA and demand checks T7-T12 are defined in the SQL files and must be run separately; the example Snowflake task is not evidence that these tests are automatically scheduled. docs/06_powerbi_report.md:55: ## Model objects
docs/06_powerbi_report.md:56:
