sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:47:   data_year VARCHAR, utility_number VARCHAR, utility_name VARCHAR, short_form VARCHAR, state VARCHAR, county VARCHAR
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:48: );
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:49: 
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:50: -- One COPY loads all 13 years because of the PATTERN. Upload the 13 .csv.gz
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:51: -- files and the two CSVs into NOAA_STAGE through Snowsight first.
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:52: COPY INTO RELIABILITY.RAW.NOAA_STORM_EVENTS FROM @RELIABILITY.RAW.NOAA_STAGE
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:53:   PATTERN = '.*StormEvents_details-ftp_v1.0_d20[0-9]{2}_c[0-9]{8}\.csv\.gz';
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:54: COPY INTO RELIABILITY.RAW.NOAA_ZONE_COUNTY FROM @RELIABILITY.RAW.NOAA_STAGE/zone_county_correlation.csv;
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:55: COPY INTO RELIABILITY.RAW.EIA861_SERVICE_TERRITORY_2025 FROM @RELIABILITY.RAW.NOAA_STAGE/Service_Territory_2025_Counties_States.csv;
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:56: 
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:57: -- Check: expected 831288, 7087, 11803
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:58: SELECT 'noaa' AS t, COUNT(*) AS n FROM RELIABILITY.RAW.NOAA_STORM_EVENTS
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:59: UNION ALL SELECT 'zones',     COUNT(*) FROM RELIABILITY.RAW.NOAA_ZONE_COUNTY
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:60: UNION ALL SELECT 'territory', COUNT(*) FROM RELIABILITY.RAW.EIA861_SERVICE_TERRITORY_2025;
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:61: 
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:62: -- ---------- 2. Staging ----------
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:63: -- Dominion Energy Virginia's counties as 5-digit FIPS codes.
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:64: -- EIA writes "Alexandria City" and NOAA writes "City of Alexandria". Both
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:65: -- spellings are tried. James City and Charles City are county names.
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:66: CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW RELIABILITY.STG.DOMINION_VA_COUNTIES AS
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:67: WITH eia AS (
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:68:   SELECT county,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:69:          CASE WHEN county LIKE '% City' THEN 'City of ' || LEFT(county, LENGTH(county) - 5) ELSE county END AS noaa_name
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:70:   FROM RELIABILITY.RAW.EIA861_SERVICE_TERRITORY_2025
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:71:   WHERE TRY_TO_NUMBER(utility_number) = 19876 AND state = 'VA'
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:72: ),
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:73: lookup AS (
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:74:   SELECT DISTINCT county_name, LPAD(fips, 5, '0') AS county_id_fips
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:75:   FROM RELIABILITY.RAW.NOAA_ZONE_COUNTY
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:76:   WHERE state = 'VA' AND fips <> '9999'
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:77: )
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:78: SELECT e.county AS eia_county, MIN(l.county_id_fips) AS county_id_fips
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:79: FROM eia e
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:80: LEFT JOIN lookup l ON LOWER(l.county_name) IN (LOWER(e.county), LOWER(e.noaa_name))
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:81: GROUP BY e.county;
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:82: 
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:83: -- One row per Virginia storm event per county it touched.
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:84: -- County events carry the county code directly. Zone events are spread over
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:85: -- the counties in that forecast zone through NOAA's own lookup.
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:86: CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW RELIABILITY.STG.NOAA_VA_EVENTS AS
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:87: SELECT
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:88:   TRY_TO_NUMBER(e.event_id)            AS event_id,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:89:   TRY_TO_NUMBER(e.episode_id)          AS episode_id,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:90:   TRY_TO_NUMBER(e.year)                AS year,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:91:   TRY_TO_NUMBER(e.begin_yearmonth)     AS begin_yearmonth,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:92:   TRY_TO_NUMBER(e.begin_day)           AS begin_day,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:93:   e.event_type,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:94:   e.cz_type,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:95:   CASE WHEN e.cz_type = 'C' THEN '51' || LPAD(e.cz_fips, 3, '0')
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:96:        ELSE COALESCE(LPAD(z.fips, 5, '0'), n.county_id_fips) END AS county_id_fips,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:97:   TRY_TO_NUMBER(e.magnitude, 8, 2)     AS magnitude,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:98:   CASE
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:99:     WHEN e.event_type IN ('Thunderstorm Wind','High Wind','Strong Wind','Tornado') THEN 'wind'
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:100:     WHEN e.event_type IN ('Ice Storm','Winter Storm','Heavy Snow','Blizzard','Sleet') THEN 'winter'
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:101:     WHEN e.event_type IN ('Hurricane','Hurricane (Typhoon)','Tropical Storm','Tropical Depression') THEN 'tropical'
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:102:     ELSE 'other'
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:103:   END AS line_risk
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:104: FROM RELIABILITY.RAW.NOAA_STORM_EVENTS e
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:105: LEFT JOIN RELIABILITY.RAW.NOAA_ZONE_COUNTY z
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:106:   ON e.cz_type = 'Z' AND z.state = 'VA' AND z.fips <> '9999'
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:107:  AND TRY_TO_NUMBER(z.zone) = TRY_TO_NUMBER(e.cz_fips)
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:108: -- second pass: zones renumbered after the lookup was built carry the county
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:109: -- name in cz_name, so match on the name when the zone number is unknown
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:110: LEFT JOIN (
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:111:   SELECT DISTINCT UPPER(REPLACE(county_name, 'City of ', '')) AS county_name, LPAD(fips, 5, '0') AS county_id_fips
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:112:   FROM RELIABILITY.RAW.NOAA_ZONE_COUNTY WHERE state = 'VA' AND fips <> '9999'
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:113: ) n ON e.cz_type = 'Z' AND z.fips IS NULL AND n.county_name = UPPER(e.cz_name)
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:114: WHERE e.state = 'VIRGINIA';
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:115: 
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:116: -- ---------- 3. Mart: the storm index, one row per year ----------
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:117: -- A storm county-day is one county with at least one line-risk event on one
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:118: -- date. Counting county-days rather than events avoids counting multiple
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:119: -- reports of one storm in one county more than once.
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:120: CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE RELIABILITY.MART.STORM_INDEX COPY GRANTS AS
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:121: WITH days AS (
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:122:   SELECT year, county_id_fips, begin_yearmonth, begin_day,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:123:          MAX(CASE WHEN line_risk = 'wind'     THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS wind,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:124:          MAX(CASE WHEN line_risk = 'winter'   THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS winter,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:125:          MAX(CASE WHEN line_risk = 'tropical' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS tropical,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:126:          MAX(CASE WHEN event_type = 'Thunderstorm Wind' THEN magnitude END) AS max_gust_kt
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:127:   FROM RELIABILITY.STG.NOAA_VA_EVENTS
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:128:   WHERE county_id_fips IN (SELECT county_id_fips FROM RELIABILITY.STG.DOMINION_VA_COUNTIES WHERE county_id_fips IS NOT NULL)
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:129:     AND line_risk <> 'other'
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:130:   GROUP BY 1, 2, 3, 4
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:131: )
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:132: SELECT year,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:133:        COUNT(*)                                          AS storm_county_days,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:134:        SUM(wind)                                         AS wind_days,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:135:        SUM(winter)                                       AS winter_days,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:136:        SUM(tropical)                                     AS tropical_days,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:137:        SUM(CASE WHEN max_gust_kt >= 60 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS severe_gust_days
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:138: FROM days
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:139: GROUP BY year
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:140: ORDER BY year;
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:141: 
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:142: -- ---------- 4. Tests ----------
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:143: -- T7. Every Dominion county found a FIPS code (expected 0 rows)
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:144: SELECT * FROM RELIABILITY.STG.DOMINION_VA_COUNTIES WHERE county_id_fips IS NULL;
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:145: 
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:146: -- T8. The 2025 file is a full year (expected one row: 202512)
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:147: SELECT MAX(begin_yearmonth) AS last_month FROM RELIABILITY.STG.NOAA_VA_EVENTS WHERE year = 2025;
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:148: 
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:149: -- T9. Zone events that found no county even after the name pass (expected 0)
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:150: SELECT COUNT(*) AS unmapped_zone_rows FROM RELIABILITY.STG.NOAA_VA_EVENTS WHERE county_id_fips IS NULL;
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:151: 
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:152: -- ---------- 5. Analysis ----------
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:153: -- Q6. Storm county-days vs Dominion's ex-MED SAIDI, by year
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:154: WITH j AS (
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:155:   SELECT s.year, s.storm_county_days, s.wind_days, s.winter_days,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:156:          f.saidi_ex_med, f.saifi_ex_med, f.storm_saidi
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:157:   FROM RELIABILITY.MART.STORM_INDEX s
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:158:   JOIN RELIABILITY.MART.FCT_RELIABILITY f ON f.year = s.year AND f.utility_key = '19876|VA'
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:159: )
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:160: SELECT * FROM j ORDER BY year;
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:161: 
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:162: -- Q6b. Correlations and the 2025 prediction from the fit
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:163: WITH j AS (
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:164:   SELECT s.year, s.storm_county_days, s.wind_days, f.saidi_ex_med, f.saifi_ex_med, f.storm_saidi
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:165:   FROM RELIABILITY.MART.STORM_INDEX s
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:166:   JOIN RELIABILITY.MART.FCT_RELIABILITY f ON f.year = s.year AND f.utility_key = '19876|VA'
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:167: ),
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:168: fit AS (
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:169:   SELECT REGR_SLOPE(saidi_ex_med, storm_county_days)     AS slope,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:170:          REGR_INTERCEPT(saidi_ex_med, storm_county_days) AS intercept,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:171:          CORR(saidi_ex_med, storm_county_days)           AS corr_daytoday,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:172:          CORR(storm_saidi,  storm_county_days)           AS corr_major_storm,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:173:          CORR(saifi_ex_med, storm_county_days)           AS corr_frequency
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:174:   FROM j WHERE year BETWEEN 2015 AND 2024
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:175: )
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:176: SELECT ROUND(corr_daytoday, 2)    AS corr_with_daytoday_minutes,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:177:        ROUND(corr_frequency, 2)   AS corr_with_frequency,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:178:        ROUND(corr_major_storm, 2) AS corr_with_major_storm_minutes,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:179:        ROUND(intercept + slope * (SELECT storm_county_days FROM j WHERE year = 2025), 1) AS predicted_2025_daytoday_saidi,
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:180:        (SELECT saidi_ex_med FROM j WHERE year = 2025) AS actual_2025_daytoday_saidi
sql/05_noaa_storms.sql:181: FROM fit;
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:1: -- =====================================================================
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:2: -- DOMINION RELIABILITY MART  |  06: EIA-930 hourly demand, the DOM zone
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:3: -- Adds one raw table, one staging view, two mart tables, three tests and
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:4: -- four analysis queries. Run after 01 to 05.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:5: --
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:6: -- Hourly demand for the PJM "DOM" subregion since July 2018. DOM is
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:7: -- Dominion's transmission zone (Virginia and north-east North Carolina,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:8: -- including co-ops served through it). Used for load growth, not outages.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:9: -- =====================================================================
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:10: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:11: USE WAREHOUSE SNOWFLAKE_LEARNING_WH;
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:12: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:13: -- ---------- 0. File format and stage (Snowflake only) ----------
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:14: CREATE OR REPLACE FILE FORMAT RELIABILITY.RAW.FF_EIA930_CSV
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:15:   TYPE = CSV
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:16:   SKIP_HEADER = 1
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:17:   FIELD_OPTIONALLY_ENCLOSED_BY = '"'
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:18:   NULL_IF = ('')
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:19:   EMPTY_FIELD_AS_NULL = TRUE;
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:20: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:21: CREATE STAGE IF NOT EXISTS RELIABILITY.RAW.EIA930_STAGE FILE_FORMAT = RELIABILITY.RAW.FF_EIA930_CSV;
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:22: -- Upload the 17 EIA930_SUBREGION_20YY_Jan_Jun.csv / Jul_Dec.csv files
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:23: -- (2018_Jul_Dec through 2026_Jul_Dec) into EIA930_STAGE through Snowsight.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:24: -- 2018_Jan_Jun does not exist: subregion reporting starts 1 July 2018.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:25: -- A 44 KB file by that name is an HTML error page. Do not load it.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:26: -- After the upload, confirm the files are there (expected 17 rows):
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:27: LIST @RELIABILITY.RAW.EIA930_STAGE;
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:28: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:29: -- ---------- 1. Raw table, all text ----------
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:30: CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE RELIABILITY.RAW.EIA930_SUBREGION (
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:31:   balancing_authority VARCHAR, data_date VARCHAR, hour_number VARCHAR,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:32:   sub_region VARCHAR, demand_mw VARCHAR, local_time_end VARCHAR, utc_time_end VARCHAR
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:33: );
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:34: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:35: -- One COPY loads all 17 files because of the PATTERN.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:36: COPY INTO RELIABILITY.RAW.EIA930_SUBREGION FROM @RELIABILITY.RAW.EIA930_STAGE
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:37:   PATTERN = '.*EIA930_SUBREGION_20[0-9]{2}_(Jan_Jun|Jul_Dec)\.csv';
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:38: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:39: -- Check: expected 6077232 in total, 72864 for PJM / DOM
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:40: SELECT 'all subregions' AS t, COUNT(*) AS n FROM RELIABILITY.RAW.EIA930_SUBREGION
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:41: UNION ALL
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:42: SELECT 'PJM DOM', COUNT(*) FROM RELIABILITY.RAW.EIA930_SUBREGION
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:43: WHERE balancing_authority = 'PJM' AND sub_region = 'DOM';
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:44: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:45: -- ---------- 2. Staging ----------
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:46: -- DOM zone only, typed.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:47: --   1. The hour is keyed on UTC so that clock-change days (23 and 25 hours)
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:48: --      do not create duplicates.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:49: --   2. Demand above 60,000 MW is set to NULL. PJM as a whole peaks near
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:50: --      150,000 MW and DOM near 25,000, so a DOM hour above 60,000 is a
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:51: --      reporting error. Two such rows exist (18 October 2021, 1.5 billion and
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:52: --      1.0 billion MW). The reported value is retained in demand_mw_reported.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:53: CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW RELIABILITY.STG.EIA930_DOM_HOURLY AS
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:54: SELECT
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:55:   TRY_TO_DATE(data_date, 'MM/DD/YYYY')                              AS data_date,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:56:   TRY_TO_NUMBER(hour_number)                                        AS hour_no,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:57:   TRY_TO_TIMESTAMP_NTZ(utc_time_end,   'MM/DD/YYYY HH12:MI:SS AM')  AS utc_ts,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:58:   TRY_TO_TIMESTAMP_NTZ(local_time_end, 'MM/DD/YYYY HH12:MI:SS AM')  AS local_ts,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:59:   TRY_TO_NUMBER(REPLACE(demand_mw, ',', ''))                        AS demand_mw_reported,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:60:   CASE WHEN TRY_TO_NUMBER(REPLACE(demand_mw, ',', '')) > 60000 THEN NULL
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:61:        ELSE TRY_TO_NUMBER(REPLACE(demand_mw, ',', '')) END          AS demand_mw
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:62: FROM RELIABILITY.RAW.EIA930_SUBREGION
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:63: WHERE balancing_authority = 'PJM' AND sub_region = 'DOM';
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:64: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:65: -- Check: expected 72864
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:66: SELECT COUNT(*) FROM RELIABILITY.STG.EIA930_DOM_HOURLY;
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:67: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:68: -- ---------- 3. Mart ----------
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:69: -- One row per year: seasonal peaks, floor, median, mean and energy.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:70: -- Winter = Dec, Jan, Feb of the same calendar year. Summer = Jun, Jul, Aug.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:71: CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE RELIABILITY.MART.DOM_LOAD_ANNUAL COPY GRANTS AS
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:72: SELECT
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:73:   YEAR(data_date)                                                   AS year,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:74:   MAX(CASE WHEN MONTH(data_date) IN (12, 1, 2) THEN demand_mw END)  AS winter_peak_mw,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:75:   MAX(CASE WHEN MONTH(data_date) IN (6, 7, 8)  THEN demand_mw END)  AS summer_peak_mw,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:76:   MAX(demand_mw)                                                    AS peak_mw,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:77:   MIN(demand_mw)                                                    AS floor_mw,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:78:   ROUND(MEDIAN(demand_mw))                                          AS median_mw,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:79:   ROUND(AVG(demand_mw))                                             AS avg_mw,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:80:   ROUND(SUM(demand_mw) / 1e6, 2)                                    AS energy_twh,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:81:   COUNT(demand_mw)                                                  AS hours_reported,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:82:   COUNT(*) - COUNT(demand_mw)                                       AS hours_missing
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:83: FROM RELIABILITY.STG.EIA930_DOM_HOURLY
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:84: GROUP BY 1;
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:85: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:86: -- The 50 highest hours on record, ranked at two reference dates: 31 December
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:87: -- 2025 (comparable with the annual report) and the load date.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:88: CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE RELIABILITY.MART.DOM_TOP50_HOURS COPY GRANTS AS
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:89: WITH ranked AS (
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:90:   SELECT data_date, hour_no, utc_ts, demand_mw,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:91:          ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY demand_mw DESC) AS rank_today,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:92:          -- hours from 2026 sort to the bottom, so the top 50 here is the top 50 to end-2025
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:93:          ROW_NUMBER() OVER (ORDER BY CASE WHEN data_date < '2026-01-01' THEN demand_mw END DESC NULLS LAST) AS rank_end_2025_raw
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:94:   FROM RELIABILITY.STG.EIA930_DOM_HOURLY
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:95:   WHERE demand_mw IS NOT NULL
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:96: )
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:97: SELECT YEAR(data_date) AS year, data_date, hour_no, utc_ts, demand_mw, rank_today,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:98:        CASE WHEN data_date < '2026-01-01' THEN rank_end_2025_raw END AS rank_end_2025
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:99: FROM ranked
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:100: WHERE rank_today <= 50 OR (data_date < '2026-01-01' AND rank_end_2025_raw <= 50);
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:101: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:102: -- Check: expected 9 rows, 2018 to 2026
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:103: SELECT * FROM RELIABILITY.MART.DOM_LOAD_ANNUAL ORDER BY year;
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:104: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:105: -- ---------- 4. Tests ----------
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:106: -- T10. Impossible demand values (expected 2 rows, both 18 Oct 2021). These are
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:107: --      what the staging view sets to NULL. Review any additional rows after a refresh.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:108: SELECT data_date, hour_no, demand_mw_reported
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:109: FROM RELIABILITY.STG.EIA930_DOM_HOURLY
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:110: WHERE demand_mw_reported > 60000;
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:111: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:112: -- T11. No hour appears twice (expected 0 rows). UTC makes this true even on
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:113: --      the March and November clock-change days.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:114: SELECT utc_ts, COUNT(*) FROM RELIABILITY.STG.EIA930_DOM_HOURLY GROUP BY 1 HAVING COUNT(*) > 1;
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:115: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:116: -- T12. Every full year has at least 8,700 reported hours (expected 0 rows).
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:117: --      2018 starts in July and 2026 is not finished, so they are excluded.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:118: SELECT year, hours_reported FROM RELIABILITY.MART.DOM_LOAD_ANNUAL
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:119: WHERE year BETWEEN 2019 AND 2025 AND hours_reported < 8700;
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:120: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:121: -- ---------- 5. Analysis ----------
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:122: -- Q7. The annual report states that 38 of the 50 highest peak loads in DOM
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:123: --     Zone history occurred after 1 January 2025. EIA's record starts in
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:124: --     July 2018, so earlier years are absent and the count is higher:
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:125: --     47 of 50 in 2025, 3 in 2024.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:126: SELECT year, COUNT(*) AS hours_in_top50, MAX(demand_mw) AS highest_mw
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:127: FROM RELIABILITY.MART.DOM_TOP50_HOURS
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:128: WHERE rank_end_2025 <= 50
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:129: GROUP BY 1 ORDER BY 1;
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:130: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:131: -- Q7b. The same count at the load date. Expect 46 of 50 in 2026.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:132: SELECT year, COUNT(*) AS hours_in_top50, MAX(demand_mw) AS highest_mw
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:133: FROM RELIABILITY.MART.DOM_TOP50_HOURS
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:134: WHERE rank_today <= 50
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:135: GROUP BY 1 ORDER BY 1;
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:136: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:137: -- Q8. Winter vs summer peak by year. 2019, 2022 and 2025 were winter-peaking.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:138: --     2026: 25,413 MW on 9 February at 08:00 vs 25,384 MW on 3 July at 19:00.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:139: SELECT year, winter_peak_mw, summer_peak_mw,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:140:        CASE WHEN winter_peak_mw > summer_peak_mw THEN 'winter' ELSE 'summer' END AS peak_season
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:141: FROM RELIABILITY.MART.DOM_LOAD_ANNUAL ORDER BY 1;
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:142: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:143: -- Q9. Growth 2019 to 2025 in peak, floor, median and energy. Round-the-clock
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:144: --     load raises the lowest hour more than the highest. Expect: peak +26%,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:145: --     floor +52%, median +32%, energy +32%.
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:146: SELECT
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:147:   ROUND(100 * (b.peak_mw   / a.peak_mw   - 1), 1) AS peak_growth_pct,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:148:   ROUND(100 * (b.floor_mw  / a.floor_mw  - 1), 1) AS floor_growth_pct,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:149:   ROUND(100 * (b.median_mw / a.median_mw - 1), 1) AS median_growth_pct,
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:150:   ROUND(100 * (b.energy_twh / a.energy_twh - 1), 1) AS energy_growth_pct
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:151: FROM RELIABILITY.MART.DOM_LOAD_ANNUAL a
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:152: JOIN RELIABILITY.MART.DOM_LOAD_ANNUAL b ON a.year = 2019 AND b.year = 2025;
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:153: 
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:154: -- ---------- 6. Power BI ----------
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:155: -- BI_READER has SELECT on future tables in MART, so DOM_LOAD_ANNUAL and
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:156: -- DOM_TOP50_HOURS are available to Power BI on the next refresh. They feed
sql/06_eia930_demand.sql:157: -- the Growth page.
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:1: -- =====================================================================
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:2: -- DOMINION RELIABILITY MART  |  07: weather-adjusted reliability and the
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:3: -- hour-of-day load profile. Run after 05 and 06.
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:4: -- =====================================================================
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:5: USE WAREHOUSE SNOWFLAKE_LEARNING_WH;
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:6: 
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:7: -- ---------- 1. Storm index joined to Dominion's reliability row ----------
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:8: -- One row per year. Source for the Weather page scatter and the fit below.
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:9: CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW RELIABILITY.MART.V_STORM_VS_SAIDI COPY GRANTS AS
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:10: SELECT s.year, s.storm_county_days, s.wind_days, s.winter_days, s.tropical_days, s.severe_gust_days,
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:11:        f.saidi_ex_med, f.saifi_ex_med, f.storm_saidi
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:12: FROM RELIABILITY.MART.STORM_INDEX s
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:13: JOIN RELIABILITY.MART.FCT_RELIABILITY f
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:14:   ON f.year = s.year AND f.utility_key = '19876|VA';
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:15: 
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:16: -- ---------- 2. Weather-adjusted SAIDI ----------
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:17: -- Fit SAIDI ex MED on storm county-days over 2015 to 2024 (decision D6:
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:18: -- 2013-14 precede the 2015 counting change and 2025 is held out so that its
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:19: -- prediction is out of sample). Weather-adjusted = actual - predicted +
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:20: -- period mean.
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:21: CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE RELIABILITY.MART.SAIDI_WEATHER_ADJUSTED COPY GRANTS AS
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:22: WITH fit AS (
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:23:   SELECT REGR_SLOPE(saidi_ex_med, storm_county_days)     AS slope,
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:24:          REGR_INTERCEPT(saidi_ex_med, storm_county_days) AS intercept,
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:25:          AVG(saidi_ex_med)                               AS mean_saidi
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:26:   FROM RELIABILITY.MART.V_STORM_VS_SAIDI
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:27:   WHERE year BETWEEN 2015 AND 2024
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:28: )
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:29: SELECT v.year, v.storm_county_days, v.saidi_ex_med,
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:30:        ROUND(f.intercept + f.slope * v.storm_county_days, 1)                                   AS predicted_from_weather,
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:31:        ROUND(v.saidi_ex_med - (f.intercept + f.slope * v.storm_county_days) + f.mean_saidi, 1) AS weather_adjusted_saidi,
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:32:        ROUND(f.slope, 4)                                                                       AS fit_slope,
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:33:        ROUND(f.intercept, 1)                                                                   AS fit_intercept,
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:34:        ROUND(f.mean_saidi, 1)                                                                  AS fit_mean
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:35: FROM RELIABILITY.MART.V_STORM_VS_SAIDI v CROSS JOIN fit f
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:36: WHERE v.year >= 2015;
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:37: 
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:38: -- Check: expected 11 rows. 2019 weather_adjusted 135.8, 2025 133.1,
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:39: -- predicted_from_weather for 2025 145.3, slope 0.0514, intercept 104.4
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:40: SELECT * FROM RELIABILITY.MART.SAIDI_WEATHER_ADJUSTED ORDER BY year;
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:41: 
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:42: -- ---------- 3. Hour-of-day load profile, 2019 vs 2025 ----------
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:43: -- Average demand in each hour of the day, 2019 vs 2025.
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:44: CREATE OR REPLACE TABLE RELIABILITY.MART.DOM_LOAD_PROFILE COPY GRANTS AS
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:45: SELECT hour_no,
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:46:        ROUND(AVG(CASE WHEN YEAR(data_date) = 2019 THEN demand_mw END)) AS avg_mw_2019,
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:47:        ROUND(AVG(CASE WHEN YEAR(data_date) = 2025 THEN demand_mw END)) AS avg_mw_2025,
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:48:        ROUND(100 * (AVG(CASE WHEN YEAR(data_date) = 2025 THEN demand_mw END)
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:49:                   / AVG(CASE WHEN YEAR(data_date) = 2019 THEN demand_mw END) - 1), 1) AS growth_pct
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:50: FROM RELIABILITY.STG.EIA930_DOM_HOURLY
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:51: WHERE hour_no BETWEEN 1 AND 24
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:52: GROUP BY 1;
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:53: 
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:54: -- Check: expected 24 rows. Hour 4 growth 41.0, hour 16 growth 27.1
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:55: SELECT * FROM RELIABILITY.MART.DOM_LOAD_PROFILE ORDER BY hour_no;
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:56: 
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:57: -- ---------- 4. Analysis ----------
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:58: -- Q10. Trend in the weather-adjusted series. Expect a slope under one
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:59: --      minute a year.
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:60: SELECT ROUND(REGR_SLOPE(weather_adjusted_saidi, year), 2) AS minutes_per_year_2015_2025,
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:61:        ROUND(AVG(CASE WHEN year BETWEEN 2015 AND 2018 THEN weather_adjusted_saidi END), 1) AS avg_2015_2018,
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:62:        ROUND(AVG(CASE WHEN year BETWEEN 2019 AND 2022 THEN weather_adjusted_saidi END), 1) AS avg_2019_2022,
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:63:        ROUND(AVG(CASE WHEN year BETWEEN 2023 AND 2025 THEN weather_adjusted_saidi END), 1) AS avg_2023_2025
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:64: FROM RELIABILITY.MART.SAIDI_WEATHER_ADJUSTED;
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:65: 
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:66: -- Q11. Daily load factor: ratio of each day's lowest hour to its highest,
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:67: --      averaged over the year. Expect 0.69 in 2019 and 0.76 in 2025.
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:68: WITH d AS (
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:69:   SELECT YEAR(data_date) AS year, data_date, MIN(demand_mw) AS lo, MAX(demand_mw) AS hi
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:70:   FROM RELIABILITY.STG.EIA930_DOM_HOURLY
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:71:   WHERE demand_mw IS NOT NULL
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:72:   GROUP BY 1, 2
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:73: )
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:74: SELECT year, ROUND(AVG(lo)) AS avg_daily_min_mw, ROUND(AVG(hi)) AS avg_daily_max_mw, ROUND(AVG(lo / hi), 3) AS avg_min_over_max
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:75: FROM d WHERE year BETWEEN 2019 AND 2025
sql/07_weather_adjusted_and_profile.sql:76: GROUP BY 1 ORDER BY 1;
sql/08_grants.sql:1: -- =====================================================================
sql/08_grants.sql:2: -- DOMINION RELIABILITY MART  |  08: read-only access for Power BI
sql/08_grants.sql:3: -- Run as ACCOUNTADMIN. Replace the password placeholder before running.
sql/08_grants.sql:4: -- Do not commit the password.
sql/08_grants.sql:5: -- =====================================================================
sql/08_grants.sql:6: 
sql/08_grants.sql:7: -- Read-only role on the mart.
sql/08_grants.sql:8: CREATE ROLE IF NOT EXISTS BI_READER;
sql/08_grants.sql:9: 
sql/08_grants.sql:10: GRANT USAGE ON DATABASE RELIABILITY                     TO ROLE BI_READER;
sql/08_grants.sql:11: GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA   RELIABILITY.MART                TO ROLE BI_READER;
sql/08_grants.sql:12: GRANT SELECT ON ALL TABLES    IN SCHEMA RELIABILITY.MART TO ROLE BI_READER;
sql/08_grants.sql:13: GRANT SELECT ON ALL VIEWS     IN SCHEMA RELIABILITY.MART TO ROLE BI_READER;
sql/08_grants.sql:14: 
sql/08_grants.sql:15: -- CREATE OR REPLACE drops grants on the object it replaces. FUTURE grants
sql/08_grants.sql:16: -- mean a rebuilt or newly added mart object is readable without re-granting.
sql/08_grants.sql:17: GRANT SELECT ON FUTURE TABLES IN SCHEMA RELIABILITY.MART TO ROLE BI_READER;
sql/08_grants.sql:18: GRANT SELECT ON FUTURE VIEWS  IN SCHEMA RELIABILITY.MART TO ROLE BI_READER;
sql/08_grants.sql:19: 
sql/08_grants.sql:20: GRANT USAGE ON WAREHOUSE SNOWFLAKE_LEARNING_WH TO ROLE BI_READER;
sql/08_grants.sql:21: 
sql/08_grants.sql:22: -- Service user for the Power BI connector. LEGACY_SERVICE is required for
sql/08_grants.sql:23: -- username and password authentication from the connector. In a shared
sql/08_grants.sql:24: -- environment this would be key-pair authentication with the key in a
sql/08_grants.sql:25: -- secrets manager.
sql/08_grants.sql:26: CREATE USER IF NOT EXISTS POWERBI_SVC
sql/08_grants.sql:27:   PASSWORD = '<set-a-strong-password-here-and-do-not-commit-it>'
sql/08_grants.sql:28:   TYPE = LEGACY_SERVICE
sql/08_grants.sql:29:   DEFAULT_ROLE = BI_READER
sql/08_grants.sql:30:   DEFAULT_WAREHOUSE = SNOWFLAKE_LEARNING_WH
sql/08_grants.sql:31:   MUST_CHANGE_PASSWORD = FALSE;
sql/08_grants.sql:32: 
sql/08_grants.sql:33: GRANT ROLE BI_READER TO USER POWERBI_SVC;
sql/08_grants.sql:34: 
sql/08_grants.sql:35: -- Checks
sql/08_grants.sql:36: SHOW GRANTS TO ROLE BI_READER;
sql/08_grants.sql:37: SHOW GRANTS TO USER POWERBI_SVC;
